{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/CHANGELOG.md",
  "old_string": "## [Unreleased]\n\n## [0.11.0] - 2026-06-28",
  "new_string": "## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- **Advancements & statistics**: the game gains a progression meta — running **statistics** (blocks mined, monsters slain, items crafted/enchanted, potions drunk, fish caught, animals bred, distance travelled, time played, deaths, jumps, villager trades) and a tight, flat set of **16 advancements**, one per major system (mine a log/stone/sliver/diamond, craft a pickaxe, craft a furnace, fire a bow, slay a hostile, defeat the Dragon Lord, breed animals, harvest wheat, catch a fish, enchant an item, drink a potion, sleep, trade with a villager). All on **zero new assets** — advancement icons reuse existing item/block sprites — and the save schema bumps **v12 → v13** (additive), with **no worldgen change** (`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays 8)\n  - Press **L** to open a dedicated two-tab overlay (mutually exclusive with the inventory; the pause menu won't open over it): an **Advancements** grid grouped by category with locked/unlocked styling, and a **Statistics** list of the counters. Each unlock raises an _\"Advancement Unlocked\"_ toast and a celebratory chime\n  - **Persist across death.** Like `xp` and unlike status `effects`, both stats and advancements are a long-term record that survives a death (`clearEffects` never touches them) and a reload\n  - **Architecture**: one observer at the engine's single `emit` chokepoint folds every gameplay event into the stat counters (`recordEvent`), then unlocks any advancement whose threshold the new stats just crossed and announces it (`advancementUnlocked` event) — no per-system bookkeeping. The whole set is a **declarative registry** in `lib/game/engine/systems/advancements.ts` (`{ stat, threshold }` per row), so `evaluateAdvancements` is pure and adding one is a one-line edit. A new broadly-useful `crafted` event drives the craft/brew/trade counters; `HOSTILE_MOB_KINDS` (`lib/game/mobs.ts`) is the single source of truth for counting hostile kills. The overlay reads detail through a new `GameApi.advancementState()` pulled on render, so play never churns the snapshot with stat values the HUD doesn't show\n\n## [0.11.0] - 2026-06-28"
}
```

===== docs/adding-content.md (headings) =====
1:# Adding content
5:## A new block
16:## A new item or recipe
31:- **Villager trades** (`lib/game/trades.ts`) ARE just `station: "villager"` recipes spread into `RECIPES`, so they reuse the whole crafting pipeline and recipe-book UI for free. To open them, an interaction sets `state.craftingStation = "villager"` (right-clicking a villager via `tryTradeAimedVillager` in `interact.ts`); the panel then shows the trades as a **Trading** book and the `craft` command runs them. Emerald is the currency — add sell offers (material → emerald) and buy offers (emerald → goods) to keep the economy two-sided. There's no persisted trade state, so this needs no save-format change.
33:- **"How to obtain" hint**: the recipe-book tooltip tells the player where a missing ingredient comes from. `lib/game/itemSources.ts` derives this once from the existing tables (recipes → "craft/smelt/trade/brew it", `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` → "mine X", `MOB_DROPS` → "hunt X", fishing/dungeon loot), so a new recipe or drop is covered automatically. Only add to `MINE_OVERRIDES` there when the dropped item differs from the block you break (e.g. coal from coal ore, or a chance drop in `rollBlockDrops` like saplings/seeds).
35:## A new mob drop
40:## A new mob
48:- **A new `MobKind` touches five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables** (typecheck enforces all of them): `MOB_TEMPLATES` (`mobs.ts`), `MOB_DROPS` (`mobLoot.ts`), `CALL_INTERVALS` (`mobAmbience.ts`), and `MOB_AMBIENT_SOUNDS` + `MOB_ATTACK_SOUNDS` (`soundParams.ts`). `soundParams.test.ts` iterates `MOB_TEMPLATES`, so the three sound/template tables must stay in lockstep.
50:## A ranged weapon or ranged mob
57:## An explosion (creeper, TNT, or anything that blows up)
64:## A new player skin preset
70:## A new sound
76:## An interactive block (right-click behavior)
81:- **A station with its own panel (not the recipe book)** — the **enchanting table**, the **anvil**, and the **grindstone** are the reference. Their interaction sets `state.craftingStation` to a new id, but that id goes on the **engine/UI** station union (`craftingStation` on `GameState` and `GameSnapshot`, and the `openedStation` event in `state.ts`, plus the `InventoryPanel` `craftingStation` prop) and **not** on `Recipe.station` — so `STATION_LABELS` (keyed off `Recipe.station`) needs no entry. The panel is a dedicated `*Column` component branched in before the recipe book in `InventoryPanel`, acting on the **selected hotbar slot** (`inventory[selectedHotbarSlot]`); its actions are new `Command`s in `commands.ts` handled in `GameEngine.dispatch` (gated on `state.craftingStation === <id>`), each emitting a one-shot event for the audio director. The block itself is **craft-only** (never generated), so adding the `BlockId` at the end of the enum needs **no `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump** — just `BLOCK_COLORS` (atlas sizing), an atlas paint branch, the exhaustive `GROUP_BY_BLOCK`, and `BREAK_HARDNESS`/item-def/`BLOCK_TO_SLOT`/recipe.
83:## A container block (block-entity storage)
91:## A random-tick behavior (growth / spread)
99:## A transient (session-only) entity (projectile, bobber, …)
105:## A worldgen structure (houses, dungeons, …)
108:- **Any block write changes the deterministic output**, so this breaks the worldgen hash. Re-baseline the `generation.test.ts` digests and bump `WORLDGEN_VERSION` per the [testing.md](testing.md) policy (each world then discards its stale block-diffs and reboots from its seed); add a structural probe test (count your blocks, assert they generate) that survives the re-baseline.
111:## A loot table / lazy block-entity fill
116:## A status effect (timed buff / hazard)
119:- **Apply it at one seam.** Each existing effect reads at exactly one place: Speed multiplies `playerMotion.ts` speed (`speedMultiplier`), Strength adds to the melee dispatch in `GameEngine.ts` (`strengthBonus`), Fire Resistance / Water Breathing are booleans passed into `tickLavaExposure`/`tickOxygen` (`playerStats.ts`), and Regeneration/Poison run inside `tickStatusEffects` on their **own** accumulators. Prefer a gate/modifier over a new tick; `tickStatusEffects` already runs after `tickHealthRegen` and before the environmental ticks, so its gates are current.
120:- **Damage that shouldn't kill** uses `applyNonLethalDamage` (`playerLife.ts`), not `applyUnmitigatedDamage` — poison floors at `POISON_FLOOR_HP`.
121:- **Grant it.** A drinkable potion is a `material` item with an `effect: { id, durationSeconds }` (`ItemDef`/`InventorySlot`); the `drinkPotion` command and the `F` key apply it via `addEffect`. A brewed potion is a `station: "brewing"` recipe (see "A new item or recipe"). A hazard source just calls `addEffect` (e.g. the rotten-flesh roll in the `eatFood` case, using the injected `this.rng`).
122:- **Show it.** Add a `HudIconName` + grid in `hudPixels.ts`; the cached `effectsProjection` in `GameEngine` feeds `GameSnapshot.activeEffects`, which `ActiveEffects.tsx` renders — keep the projection ref-stable (rebuild only when rounded seconds change) so the HUD doesn't thrash.
123:- **Persist it.** Effects are an **additive save bump** — see [save-format.md](save-format.md) (v6); they're cleared on death and `restoreEffects` validates them on load.
125:## An enchantment (per-item gear modifier)
128:- **Bind to one item, not a whole kind**: an `ENCHANTMENT_DEFS` entry can carry an optional `itemIds` allow-list — when set, `canEnchant` also requires the slot's item id to be in it. This is how **Power/Punch** apply to the **bow** only and not to swords (which are `weapon` too). The seam reader is the same `enchantLevel(slot, id) × magnitude` shape; for the bow it's read in `tryFireBow` (`combat.ts`).
129:- **Read it at one seam**, the same as a status effect: add a reader (`sharpnessBonus`, `efficiencyMultiplier`, …) and call it where the value is computed — melee damage (the `GameEngine` attack dispatch), `equippedDefense`, `miningSpeed`, or the durability helpers (Unbreaking threads an optional `rng` into `consumeToolDurability`/`consumeEquippedArmorDurability` and returns `null`/unchanged to skip a point of wear).
130:- **Non-stackable for free**: enchantments only sit on durable gear, which is already stack-size 1 (`maxStackSizeForItem`), and `{ ...slot }` clones carry the field through every inventory move.
131:- **Apply it** with the `enchant` command (gated on an open enchanting table): `canEnchant` (kind + level cap) → `spendXpLevels` → immutable `applyEnchant`. The `EnchantingColumn` panel renders the options for the selected hotbar item.
132:- **Persist it**: enchantments are an **additive save field** on `SavedSlot` — see [save-format.md](save-format.md) (v7); `restoreSlot` validates them on load (known ids, clamped levels, durable gear only).
134:## A new mechanic
===== docs/testing.md (headings) =====
1:# Testing
5:- **`bun test`** — unit, integration, and component tests, colocated as `*.test.ts[x]` next to the code they cover. The shared setup (`tests/setup.ts`, preloaded via `bunfig.toml`) registers happy-dom so React component tests run under the same runner; the game engine itself needs no DOM (see [architecture.md](architecture.md)).
6:- **`bun run test:e2e`** — Playwright browser smoke tests in `e2e/*.e2e.ts` against the production build. The file suffix matters: `bun test` auto-collects `*.test.ts` AND `*.spec.ts`, so E2E files must use `.e2e.ts`.

54	## Controls
55	
56	| Key / input        | Action                                                                                                        |
57	| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
58	| `W` `A` `S` `D`    | Move (walk / strafe)                                                                                          |
59	| `Space`            | Jump                                                                                                          |
60	| `C`                | Crouch (slower, careful movement)                                                                             |
61	| `W` + `CapsLock`   | Sprint — faster, but drains hunger                                                                            |
62	| Double-tap `Space` | Toggle flight in Creative (Spectator always flies); `Space` / `C` rise/descend while flying                   |
63	| Mouse              | Look around (double-click the game first to lock the pointer)                                                 |
64	| Left-click (hold)  | Break the targeted block / attack a mob                                                                       |
65	| Right-click or `E` | Place/interact, or throw a selected spear                                                                     |
66	| `1`–`9`            | Select a hotbar slot                                                                                          |
67	| `I`                | Open / close inventory & crafting                                                                             |
68	| `F`                | Eat the selected food, or drink a selected potion                                                             |
69	| `V`                | Cycle camera: first-person → third-person rear → third-person front                                           |

72	| `F3`               | Debug overlay (FPS, position, daylight, mob counts)                                                           |

75	audio follow where your eyes point, and the crosshair stays centered.

340	
341	## Experience and enchanting
342	
343	Playing earns **XP**, shown as a green bar above the hotbar with your **level**
344	over it. You gain XP for **killing mobs** (tougher mobs give more — the boss is a
345	big payout), **mining ores** (rarer ores give more), and **reeling in fish**. XP
346	is a currency, so unlike potion effects it **stays with you when you die**.
347	
348	Spend it at an **enchanting table** (craft one from **2 diamond ore + 4 cobble**).
349	Place it, right-click to open the enchanting panel, then **select** the tool,
350	weapon, or armor you want to improve in your hotbar — the panel shows the
351	enchantments it can take. Each costs **3 levels** and can be applied up to **three
352	times**:
353	
354	- **Sharpness** (weapons) — hit harder in melee
355	- **Knockback** (weapons) — your melee hits shove mobs further back
356	- **Looting** (weapons) — kills drop more loot
357	- **Power** (bows) — arrows hit harder
358	- **Punch** (bows) — arrows knock the target back further
359	- **Protection** (armor) — take less damage
360	- **Feather Falling** (boots) — take less fall damage
361	- **Efficiency** (tools) — mine faster
362	- **Fortune** (tools) — ore drops more of itself when you mine it
363	- **Unbreaking** (any gear) — gear lasts longer (it sometimes ignores wear)
364	- **Mending** (any gear) — XP you pick up repairs the held or worn item instead of
365	  banking, as long as it's damaged
366	
367	Buttons grey out when you can't afford the cost or the enchant is maxed.
368	Enchantments are part of the item, so they're kept in chests and across reloads.
369	Full numbers are in the [reference](reference.md#xp--enchanting).
370	
371	## The anvil
372	
373	Craft an **anvil** (**3 gold ore + 4 cobble**), place it, and right-click to open
374	the anvil panel. Like the enchanting table it works on the **selected** hotbar
375	item — a tool, weapon, or armor piece — and spends XP levels:
376	
377	- **Combine** a second copy of the same item into it. The result keeps the best of
378	  both: its durability is restored (the two bars added together, plus a bonus) and
379	  any enchantments are merged, taking the higher level of each. The duplicate is
380	  used up. Costs **4 levels**.
381	- **Repair** with raw material — feed it the item's tier material (e.g. a diamond
382	  sword takes **diamond ore**, a stone pickaxe takes **cobble**) to restore a chunk
383	  of durability without needing a second copy. Costs **1 level** per material unit.
384	- **Rename** the item to anything you like (it shows everywhere the item's name
385	  does). Costs **1 level**.
386	
387	Buttons grey out when there's nothing to do (no duplicate, full durability, or no
388	matching material) or you can't afford the cost. For passive, no-table upkeep,
389	enchant gear with **Mending** instead.
390	
391	## The grindstone
392	
393	Craft a **grindstone** (**2 cobble + 2 planks**) and right-click it to open the
394	grindstone panel — the anvil's opposite. With an **enchanted** tool, weapon, or
395	armor selected in your hotbar, **Remove enchantments** strips them all off and
396	**refunds XP** (5 points per enchantment level removed). Handy for salvaging XP
397	from gear you've outgrown, or clearing an enchant you don't want before
398	re-enchanting. It costs nothing to use — it only gives XP back.
399	
```

399	
400	## Doors
401	
402	Craft a **wood door** from **6 planks** and place it on a solid floor with two
403	clear blocks above it. A door is a thin, two-block-tall panel: right-click either
404	half to open or close the whole door. Closed doors block players and mobs; open
405	doors rotate against their hinge so you can pass. Mobs cannot open or close doors.
406	Breaking either half removes the whole door and returns one door item.
407	
408	## Storage
409	
410	Your pack only holds 36 slots, so build **chests** to stash the overflow. Craft a
411	chest from **8 planks**, place it, and **right-click** it to open it: a **27-slot**
412	grid appears above your inventory. Move items the same way you rearrange the
413	inventory — **click a slot, then click where it should go** — across either grid.
414	Close the panel (`I` or `Esc`) and the chest keeps its contents, which are **saved
415	with your world**.
416	
417	Breaking a chest doesn't destroy what's inside: its contents **spill back into your
418	inventory** (and you get the chest item back). If your inventory is too full to hold
419	everything, the break is refused and the chest stays put — empty it a bit first.
420	
421	## Mobs and breeding
422	
423	Ten creatures roam the world:
424	
425	- **Passive** — **sheep**, **chicken**, **horse**, **cow**, **pig**. They wander,
426	  flee when you get close, and never attack. They drop materials and raw meat when
427	  killed — cows give leather and beef, pigs give porkchops.
428	- **Villager** — passive but **doesn't flee**, so you can walk right up and
429	  **right-click to trade** (see [Trading](#trading)). It drops nothing if killed —
430	  no reason to harm your shopkeeper.
431	- **Hostile** — **zombie**, **skeleton**, **spider**, **creeper**. They hunt you at
432	  night, chase within their detection range, and attack when they have line of sight.
433	  Every hostile has **100 HP**, shown on a health bar above its head.
434	  **Skeletons are archers** — they keep their distance and fire arrows, so close in
435	  or take cover. **Creepers** sneak up and **explode** — they light a short fuse when
436	  they get close (you'll hear the hiss and see them swell), then blow a crater and
437	  hurt you badly. Back away to defuse one, or kill it first for its gunpowder.
438	  Spiders are only hostile in the dark. Nothing spawns within 16 blocks of you.
```

434	## Dungeons
435	
436	Small cobblestone rooms (speckled with mossy cobble) generate **underground**,
437	well clear of the spawn area. Each holds 1–2 **loot chests** and a central **mob
438	spawner**. The loot tables live in `lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts`; a chest fills on
439	first open or break, with loot seeded so it's the same until you reach it.
440	
441	Each chest rolls a tier:
442	
443	| Tier   | Odds | Contents                                                                                                                      |
444	| ------ | ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
445	| Common | 75%  | Bone (always), bread, cooked chicken, string, seeds, sliver/gold ore, an occasional stone pickaxe                             |
446	| Rare   | 25%  | Everything a common chest rolls **plus** diamond/sapphire ore, a ruby/sapphire sword, helmet/chestplate, rare diamond pickaxe |
447	
448	**Spawners** are time-independent: while you're within ~16 blocks, a spawner
449	drips one hostile (zombie/skeleton/spider) every ~8 s onto the room floor, up to
450	6 clustered nearby (and never past the global hostile cap of 16). Mining the
451	spawner block out stops it.
452	
453	## Endgame
454	
455	Craft a **Cursed Totem** (1 diamond ore + 2 bone + 2 gold ore) and right-click
456	it in the open to summon the **Boss** nearby (refused if one already walks). The
457	fight is meant to test full diamond gear plus a bow:
458	
459	- It approaches and **bites** for 10 up close, looses a **3-arrow spread** for 7
460	  each at range, and periodically **summons** a skeleton or zombie (up to 4, under
461	  the global cap). It has **1000 HP** and does not burn in daylight.
462	- Every hostile shows a health bar above its head. The boss also has a
463	  top-of-screen health bar while it lives.
464	- Defeating it drops a **Dragon Heart** (+ 2–4 diamond ore), shows a one-time
465	  **victory screen**, and lets you craft the **Dragon Sword** (60 attack). You
466	  keep playing afterward; another totem summons it again.
467	
```

  "new_string": "- Defeating it drops a **Dragon Heart** (+ 2–4 diamond ore), shows a one-time\n  **victory screen**, and lets you craft the **Dragon Sword** (60 attack). You\n  keep playing afterward; another totem summons it again.\n\n## Advancements\n\nPress **L** to open the advancements & statistics overlay. Advancements unlock\nthe moment their goal is met (a toast + chime), persist in the save, and are\n**kept across death**. The registry lives in `lib/game/engine/systems/advancements.ts`;\neach is a declarative `{ stat, threshold }` row, so nothing is gated behind them.\n\n| Advancement    | Category  | How to unlock              |\n| -------------- | --------- | -------------------------- |\n| Getting Wood   | Mining    | Chop down a log            |\n| Stone Age      | Mining    | Mine a block of stone      |\n| Iron-ish       | Mining    | Mine sliver ore            |\n| Diamonds!      | Mining    | Mine diamond ore           |\n| Tool Up        | Crafting  | Craft a pickaxe (any tier) |\n| Hot Topic      | Crafting  | Craft a furnace            |\n| Take Aim       | Combat    | Fire an arrow from a bow   |\n| Monster Hunter | Combat    | Slay a hostile monster     |\n| Dragon Slayer  | Combat    | Defeat the Dragon Lord     |\n| Two by Two     | Farming   | Breed two animals          |\n| Time to Farm   | Farming   | Harvest fully-grown wheat  |\n| Gone Fishing   | Farming   | Reel in a catch            |\n| Enchanter      | Magic     | Enchant an item            |\n| Local Brewery  | Magic     | Drink a potion             |\n| Sleep Tight    | Adventure | Sleep through the night    |\n| Hired Help     | Adventure | Trade with a villager      |\n\n## Statistics\n\nRunning counters shown on the overlay's Statistics tab — also persisted and kept\nacross death. (Internally more counters are tracked to drive advancements, e.g.\nper-ore mining and per-recipe crafts; these are the ones displayed.)\n\n| Statistic          | Counts                                       |\n| ------------------ | -------------------------------------------- |\n| Time Played        | Seconds of active play                       |\n| Distance Travelled | Blocks moved                                 |\n| Blocks Mined       | Total blocks broken                          |\n| Logs Chopped       | Logs broken                                  |\n| Diamond Ore Mined  | Diamond ore broken                           |\n| Monsters Slain     | Hostile mobs killed                          |\n| Animals Bred       | Successful breedings                         |\n| Items Crafted      | Successful crafts (incl. smelting & trading) |\n| Items Enchanted    | Enchantments applied                         |\n| Potions Drunk      | Potions consumed                             |\n| Fish Caught        | Fishing catches                              |\n| Arrows Fired       | Bow shots                                    |\n| Villager Trades    | Trades made with a villager                  |\n| Jumps              | Times jumped                                 |\n| Deaths             | Times died                                   |"
}

116	## A status effect (timed buff / hazard)
117	
118	- Effects live in `state.effects: Map<EffectId, number>` (id → remaining seconds), driven by `lib/game/engine/systems/statusEffects.ts`. Add an id to the `EffectId` union (`lib/game/types.ts`) and to `EFFECT_ORDER` + the `clearEffects`/helper logic; durations/strengths go in `config.ts`.
119	- **Apply it at one seam.** Each existing effect reads at exactly one place: Speed multiplies `playerMotion.ts` speed (`speedMultiplier`), Strength adds to the melee dispatch in `GameEngine.ts` (`strengthBonus`), Fire Resistance / Water Breathing are booleans passed into `tickLavaExposure`/`tickOxygen` (`playerStats.ts`), and Regeneration/Poison run inside `tickStatusEffects` on their **own** accumulators. Prefer a gate/modifier over a new tick; `tickStatusEffects` already runs after `tickHealthRegen` and before the environmental ticks, so its gates are current.
120	- **Damage that shouldn't kill** uses `applyNonLethalDamage` (`playerLife.ts`), not `applyUnmitigatedDamage` — poison floors at `POISON_FLOOR_HP`.
121	- **Grant it.** A drinkable potion is a `material` item with an `effect: { id, durationSeconds }` (`ItemDef`/`InventorySlot`); the `drinkPotion` command and the `F` key apply it via `addEffect`. A brewed potion is a `station: "brewing"` recipe (see "A new item or recipe"). A hazard source just calls `addEffect` (e.g. the rotten-flesh roll in the `eatFood` case, using the injected `this.rng`).
122	- **Show it.** Add a `HudIconName` + grid in `hudPixels.ts`; the cached `effectsProjection` in `GameEngine` feeds `GameSnapshot.activeEffects`, which `ActiveEffects.tsx` renders — keep the projection ref-stable (rebuild only when rounded seconds change) so the HUD doesn't thrash.
123	- **Persist it.** Effects are an **additive save bump** — see [save-format.md](save-format.md) (v6); they're cleared on death and `restoreEffects` validates them on load.
124	
125	## An enchantment (per-item gear modifier)
126	
127	- Enchantments are **per-item-instance data, like durability** — they live on the `InventorySlot`'s `enchantments` field (`{ id, level }[]`), driven by `lib/game/enchantments.ts`. Add an id to the `EnchantmentId` union (`types.ts`), list it in `ENCHANTMENT_DEFS` (applicable item kinds + max level) and `ENCHANTMENT_ORDER`, and put magnitudes in `config.ts`.
128	- **Bind to one item, not a whole kind**: an `ENCHANTMENT_DEFS` entry can carry an optional `itemIds` allow-list — when set, `canEnchant` also requires the slot's item id to be in it. This is how **Power/Punch** apply to the **bow** only and not to swords (which are `weapon` too). The seam reader is the same `enchantLevel(slot, id) × magnitude` shape; for the bow it's read in `tryFireBow` (`combat.ts`).
129	- **Read it at one seam**, the same as a status effect: add a reader (`sharpnessBonus`, `efficiencyMultiplier`, …) and call it where the value is computed — melee damage (the `GameEngine` attack dispatch), `equippedDefense`, `miningSpeed`, or the durability helpers (Unbreaking threads an optional `rng` into `consumeToolDurability`/`consumeEquippedArmorDurability` and returns `null`/unchanged to skip a point of wear).
130	- **Non-stackable for free**: enchantments only sit on durable gear, which is already stack-size 1 (`maxStackSizeForItem`), and `{ ...slot }` clones carry the field through every inventory move.
131	- **Apply it** with the `enchant` command (gated on an open enchanting table): `canEnchant` (kind + level cap) → `spendXpLevels` → immutable `applyEnchant`. The `EnchantingColumn` panel renders the options for the selected hotbar item.
132	- **Persist it**: enchantments are an **additive save field** on `SavedSlot` — see [save-format.md](save-format.md) (v7); `restoreSlot` validates them on load (known ids, clamped levels, durable gear only).
133	
134	## A new mechanic
135	
136	Add a system module under `lib/game/engine/systems/` (a function over `GameState`), give it a slot in the `GameEngine.step` sequence, and put its tunables in `lib/game/config.ts`. If the UI triggers it, add a `Command` variant. Write its headless test next to it.
137

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/adding-content.md",
  "old_string": "## A new mechanic\n\nAdd a system module under `lib/game/engine/systems/` (a function over `GameState`), give it a slot in the `GameEngine.step` sequence, and put its tunables in `lib/game/config.ts`. If the UI triggers it, add a `Command` variant. Write its headless test next to it.",
  "new_string": "## An advancement / a stat\n\nProgression meta lives in one declarative module, `lib/game/engine/systems/advancements.ts`, hooked to the single `GameEngine.emit` chokepoint — so you never edit per-system files.\n\n- **A new statistic.** In `recordEvent`, map the relevant `GameEvent` to a counter with `bump(state, \"<stat_id>\")` (one `Map<string, number>` on `GameState.stats`); for a tick-driven total use `recordTick`. To surface it on the Statistics tab, add a `{ id, label, format }` row to `STATS` (`format` is `\"count\" | \"distance\" | \"duration\"`). The id set is open — no allow-list to touch. If the event you need doesn't exist yet, emit a new one (the `crafted` event was added this way); every emit flows through the observer.\n- **A new advancement.** Add a row to the `ADVANCEMENTS` registry: `{ id, title, description, icon, category, stat, threshold }`. Unlock is uniform (`state.stats.get(stat) >= threshold`), so there's **no logic** — pick (or add) the `stat` it keys on and a `category` from `ADVANCEMENT_CATEGORY_ORDER`. `icon` is any existing item/block id (rendered via `itemIconUrl` — **zero new assets**; verify it renders). The engine's `observeProgress` auto-unlocks it, fires the `advancementUnlocked` toast + chime, and `AdvancementsPanel` shows it.\n- **Persist it.** `stats` and `advancements` are **additive save fields** — see [save-format.md](save-format.md) (v13). Both are kept across death (like `xp`, unlike `effects`); `restoreStats`/`restoreAdvancements` validate them on load. No new field is needed for a stat or advancement that keys on existing counters.\n- **Reuse lookups.** Counting hostile kills reads `HOSTILE_MOB_KINDS` (`lib/game/mobs.ts`), the single source of truth (hostility isn't on `MOB_TEMPLATES`) — reuse such a table rather than re-deriving it.\n\n## A new mechanic\n\nAdd a system module under `lib/game/engine/systems/` (a function over `GameState`), give it a slot in the `GameEngine.step` sequence, and put its tunables in `lib/game/config.ts`. If the UI triggers it, add a `Command` variant. Write its headless test next to it."
}

43	| Browser E2E           | `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Real browser (booting through the profile/world menu via the seeded fixture): boot without console errors, scene draws, Dragon Lord HUD direction/distance, double-click pointer lock, input → movement, craft via UI, mining, camera view cycling, skin pick persisting across reload, pause freeze/resume, save via pause menu across reload                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
44	| Browser E2E (menus)   | `e2e/menu.e2e.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Real browser from empty storage: create a world with a fixed seed and play it; create a Superflat world and confirm level terrain near spawn; Save & Quit to Worlds then switch worlds with no page reload; reload resumes the tab's world; a second profile gets its own world list                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
45	| Game modes            | `lib/game/gameModes.test.ts`, `lib/game/engine/gameMode{Creative,Spectator,Adventure,Switch}.test.ts`                                                                                                                                                         | The mode predicate table (every mode's gates) and the v7→v8 save migration / round-trip; Creative invulnerability, no-hunger, instant free-build, no-consume placement, flight toggle + fly physics, and the all-items palette give; Spectator noclip-through-a-wall, invulnerable, no-break / no-inventory, and hostiles ignoring it (vs attacking in survival); Adventure takes damage but can't edit blocks yet still swings; a mode switch resets bars / hazard timers / flight, unstucks on noclip exit, and persists through save                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
46	
47	## Running the E2E suite
48	
49	```bash
50	bunx playwright install chromium   # once
51	bun run test:e2e                   # builds and starts the prod server itself
52	```
53	
54	E2E tests assert through `window.__monecraft` (the live engine/renderer/input handle — also handy in the browser console) instead of pixels; the one render check uses `renderer.renderedTriangles()`. The shared `gamePage` fixture seeds a default profile + world and enters it through the menu (reloads auto-resume the tab's world), then fails any test that logs a console error; `menu.e2e.ts` instead drives the menus from empty storage with the plain Playwright `test`.
55	
56	**happy-dom has no 2D canvas context**: generated sprites degrade to a transparent data URL in component tests — assert sprite pixels in the pure `lib/ui` tests and component structure via roles/labels/`data-icon` attributes, not image sources.
57	
58	**happy-dom has no `AudioContext`** (and the `zzfx` package instantiates one at import): everything above the `SynthBackend` seam is pure and tested with a recording fake (see `audioDirector.test.ts`); the WebAudio glue itself (`synth.ts`, `musicPlayer.ts`, `rainLoop.ts`) plus real synthesis quality belong to the manual gameplay pass.
59	
60	**Known limitation:** headless Chromium cannot engage pointer lock (`requestPointerLock` never resolves). `acquirePointerLock` in `e2e/helpers.ts` tries the real thing, then falls back to forcing the input controller's lock flag — so key/mouse → engine wiring is still tested end to end, but real lock acquisition and look-around feel remain in the manual gameplay pass, along with visual quality. That manual pass is now only required for changes to pointer-lock handling or visual appearance.
61	
62	## The worldgen hash policy
63	
64	Saves store only the world seed plus block-change deltas, so `generateWorld` must produce byte-identical output for a given seed forever. The digests in `generation.test.ts` enforce that.
65	
66	**If a hash test fails after your change, your change breaks every existing save. Fix the code, never the hash.**
67	
68	Re-baselining the digests is legitimate in exactly two cases:
69

=== architecture.md: emit / event / observer ===
36:  progress, timers) plus `FrameInput`, `GameSnapshot`, `GameEvent`.
60:**Endgame boss & victory.** A `"boss"` `MobKind` is summoned from a held Cursed Totem via a step in the `placeBlock` precedence (`GameEngine.trySummonBoss`, which seeds placement and refuses if a boss already exists). Defeating it (`removeMobAt`) drops a Dragon Heart, sets the session-only `state.victory`, and emits `bossDefeated`. Boss HP, player-relative bearing, horizontal block distance, and the victory flag reach React through `GameSnapshot.boss`/`victory`; the `boss` object is rebuilt only when one of its rounded visible values changes, avoiding sub-block React churn. Renderer-side hostile health bars are procedural `THREE.Sprite`s attached to each mob model and updated directly from `MobState.hp`, so ordinary mob damage does not enter the React snapshot. A new `"boss"` kind requires entries in all five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables (`MOB_TEMPLATES`, `MOB_DROPS`, `CALL_INTERVALS`, `MOB_AMBIENT_SOUNDS`, `MOB_ATTACK_SOUNDS`).
62:**Game modes.** The four modes (`survival`/`creative`/`adventure`/`spectator`, `lib/game/gameModes.ts`) are a single `state.gameMode` axis. Systems gate on **intent predicates** rather than the raw id, so all four share one set of seams: `takesDamage` switches off the three damage chokepoints in `playerLife.ts` (covering fall/void/lava/water/drown/mob/poison at once) plus hunger/regen/oxygen in `playerStats.ts`; `canEditBlocks`/`freeBuild` gate breaking and placing in `mining.ts`; `canInteract` gates the interaction commands in `dispatch`; `canFly`/`isNoclip` drive flight and the collision skip in `playerMotion.ts` (and the auto-unstuck skip in `step`); `mobsThreaten` folds into `activeHostile` in `mobAI.ts`; `usesInventory`/`takesDamage` hide HUD elements. `gameMode` rides the per-world `worldType` plumbing (`WorldMeta` → a v8 `SaveData` field → `GameEngine`), but unlike `worldType` it's **switchable in-game** — `setGameMode` (`GameEngine.switchGameMode`) resets transient hazard timers, bars, flight, and noclip-entombment on the change. The transient `isFlying` is never serialized.
94:- `audioDirector.ts` — the shell-side observer, exactly parallel to the renderer: `handleEvent(GameEvent)` plays one-shots (block break/place by material, hurt/eat/jump/land, mob attacks, death/respawn stingers) and `sync(state, dt)` drives everything continuous (footsteps, mining hit ticks, mob ambience, music mood, and the weather rain bed). Music follows `state.daylight` immediately and the biome with 5 s hysteresis, and ducks while paused. The rain bed (`rainLoop.ts`) is a looping filtered-noise source on the graph, off the SFX voice budget, whose gain tracks `state.weather` rain intensity (silent for snow / while paused). The engine never imports this module.
122:- `lighting.ts` — per-voxel light (sky + block, packed 0–15 each into `world.light`). `computeFullLight` bakes the whole field from the blocks at load (top-down sunlight column pass + BFS bleed; block-light BFS from emitters); `applyEdit` patches it locally on each block edit with a Minecraft-style remove/refill flood. **Derived cache, never serialized** — a pure function of `world.blocks`. `opacity`/`emission` classify each block (air/glass transmit, water/leaves attenuate, torches/lava emit).
134:- **Lighting is a derived cache, never serialized**: `world.light` is a pure function of `world.blocks`, baked once at load (`computeFullLight`) and patched per edit (`applyEdit`), both in `lib/world/lighting.ts`. The bake/patch is wired in the **engine** (the constructor and `blockChanges.set`, the single chokepoint all gameplay edits flow through) — never the renderer — so the minimap-before-renderer `worldMeshDirty` ordering stays intact. Every new block needs a lighting class in `opacity`/`emission` (the default is opaque, no emission); sky light falls straight down a clear column for free at any level, so the bake's column pass and the incremental flood must agree (see `propagated`). `applyEdit`'s correctness is pinned by whole-array equivalence tests against a from-scratch bake.
=== tuning.md mentions advancement/stat? ===

209:nights and more pressure. The three daylight thresholds are a **documented engine
398:- **Daylight thresholds and `WAKE_DAY_PHASE`** shape the persisted `dayClock`'s

30	
31	## Game engine (`lib/game/engine/`)
32	
33	- `GameEngine.ts` — owns `GameState`, processes `dispatch(Command)`, advances `step(dt, input)`, serializes saves, and publishes snapshots (`subscribe`/`getSnapshot`). Randomness is injectable (`rng`) and the world size is overridable for fast headless tests.
34	- `state.ts` — `GameState` (player with `yaw`/`pitch`, inventory, mobs and thrown
35	  spears as **logical entities with no Three.js objects**, day clock, mining
36	  progress, timers) plus `FrameInput`, `GameSnapshot`, `GameEvent`.
37	- `commands.ts` — the `Command` union: every UI/input mutation enters the simulation through exactly this door.
38	- `blockChanges.ts` — the delta tracker behind the save format: tracked block writes against the worldgen baseline; reverted edits drop out of the save.
39	- `systems/` — one module per mechanic, each a function over `GameState`:
40	
41	### Per-frame step order (in `GameEngine.step`)
42	
43	0. Pause gate: while `state.paused`, `step` refreshes the snapshot and returns — mobs, the day clock, mining, and stats all freeze (autosave still serializes fine)
44	1. Stuck detection / auto-unstuck (`STUCK_RESET_SECONDS`)
45	2. Death check + respawn countdown (while dead, only mobs tick)
46	3. Sleep gate: while `state.sleepTimer > 0`, `step` decrements the fade and returns (full freeze, like pause); at zero it skips the clock to the next morning
47	4. Player movement & physics (`systems/playerMotion.ts` — derives direction from `yaw`, scratch vectors, no per-frame allocations)
48	5. Hunger drain, health regen, continuous-water exposure, lava burn, and oxygen/drowning damage (`systems/playerStats.ts` — `tickWaterExposure`, `tickLavaExposure`, `tickOxygen`, all on the armor-bypassing path)
49	6. Mining progress and block breaking (`systems/mining.ts`; placement also lives here)
50	7. Day-night clock (`systems/dayNight.ts` — `daylightAt()` is the single daylight formula); immediately after, `systems/weather.ts` sets the **transient** `state.weather` (rain/snow/clear) as a pure function of `dayClock` + seed — cosmetic only: never serialized, never touches spawn/daylight balance
51	8. Random block ticks (`systems/randomTicks.ts` — each interval samples columns near the player and runs per-block handlers; drives crop growth and is the extension point for future saplings / grass spread)
52	9. Night hostile spawning (`systems/spawnDirector.ts`, interval/cap in config)
53	10. Dungeon spawners (`systems/spawnDirector.ts` `tickSpawnerDirector` — each interval, every intact dungeon spawner with the player in range drips one hostile onto the room floor, bounded by a local cluster cap and the shared `HOSTILE_CAP`; time-independent)
54	11. Mob AI: wander/aggro/flee, attacks with line-of-sight, daylight burn (`systems/mobAI.ts`). Ranged mobs (skeletons, boss) kite and loose arrows instead of meleeing; the boss also bears down, melees up close, fires a spread, and summons minions
55	12. Projectiles (`systems/projectileAI.ts` `tickProjectiles` — arrows integrate under gravity in capped substeps; block hits reuse the swept world DDA and the entity test is substepped so fast arrows can't tunnel; spent arrows despawn). Also ticked in the dead branch so in-flight arrows clear
56	13. Animal breeding (`systems/breeding.ts` — feed-to-love timers, baby maturity, pairing in-love adults within range; the passive cap bounds the population)
57	
58	**Projectiles** are session-only entities (`ProjectileState` in `state.ts`, spawned via `projectiles.ts` `spawnArrow`), never serialized — like mobs. A `fromPlayer` flag is the hit filter: player arrows (the bow) hit mobs with knockback, mob arrows hit the player (armor-mitigated), and neither hits the firer. The bow, ranged skeletons, and the boss all share this one launch path. Thrown **spears** are a parallel transient entity (`ThrownSpearState`, `systems/spears.ts`) with their own gravity, swept mob collision, and terrain raycasts.
59	
```

62	**Game modes.** The four modes (`survival`/`creative`/`adventure`/`spectator`, `lib/game/gameModes.ts`) are a single `state.gameMode` axis. Systems gate on **intent predicates** rather than the raw id, so all four share one set of seams: `takesDamage` switches off the three damage chokepoints in `playerLife.ts` (covering fall/void/lava/water/drown/mob/poison at once) plus hunger/regen/oxygen in `playerStats.ts`; `canEditBlocks`/`freeBuild` gate breaking and placing in `mining.ts`; `canInteract` gates the interaction commands in `dispatch`; `canFly`/`isNoclip` drive flight and the collision skip in `playerMotion.ts` (and the auto-unstuck skip in `step`); `mobsThreaten` folds into `activeHostile` in `mobAI.ts`; `usesInventory`/`takesDamage` hide HUD elements. `gameMode` rides the per-world `worldType` plumbing (`WorldMeta` → a v8 `SaveData` field → `GameEngine`), but unlike `worldType` it's **switchable in-game** — `setGameMode` (`GameEngine.switchGameMode`) resets transient hazard timers, bars, flight, and noclip-entombment on the change. The transient `isFlying` is never serialized.
63	
64	**Difficulty.** Orthogonal to the mode, `state.difficulty` (`peaceful`/`easy`/`normal`/`hard`, `lib/game/difficulties.ts`) decides _how hard_ survival is — it follows the same shape as game modes: an enum, an `isDifficulty` guard, `DIFFICULTY_PRESETS` for the UI, and **accessor functions** (`hostilesSpawn`, `mobDamageMultiplier`, `hostileSpawnIntervalScale`, `hostileCapScale`, `regenIntervalScale`, `starves`/`starvationFloorHp`) that systems read instead of the raw id. The seams: `spawnDirector.ts` gates the initial population + night director + dungeon spawners on `hostilesSpawn` and scales the cadence/cap; `mobAI.ts` multiplies every mob hit at the strike (templates untouched); `playerStats.ts` scales regen and runs the new **starvation** tick (hunger 0 chips to a per-level HP floor, lethal only on Hard, routed through the same floored/lethal damage paths as Poison/environment). Difficulty rides the same per-world plumbing as `gameMode` (`WorldMeta` → a v9 `SaveData` field → `GameEngine`) and is switchable in-game — `setDifficulty` (`GameEngine.switchDifficulty`) just updates the value the directors read next tick, except dropping to Peaceful, which despawns the hostiles already present (a plain splice — no loot/XP, no false boss victory). The two axes don't cross-couple: spawning keys only on difficulty, threat/damage only on mode.
65	
66	**Hardcore.** A per-world boolean (`state.hardcore`, persisted in save v10 + `WorldMeta`, **immutable** unlike mode/difficulty). The constructor resolves it first so it overrides the other axes — forced to Survival + Hard — and `switchGameMode`/`switchDifficulty` early-return under it (the player-facing switchers are disabled; the engine's own death transition sets the mode directly, bypassing the lock). Death is permanent: the two lethal seams (`applyDamage`, `applyEnvironmentalDamage`) divert to `GameEngine.triggerGameOver()`, which sets the persisted `gameOver` flag and flips the player to **Spectator** (a free-cam over the dead world, invulnerable so mobs can't re-trigger it) instead of arming the respawn countdown — so a reloaded `gameOver` world boots straight into spectating (the constructor resolves `gameMode` to spectator; the existing `!isNoclip` stuck-check leaves it un-teleported). The shell force-saves on the `gameOver` event so a tab-close still persists the dead world; the Game Over screen (`components/game/GameOverScreen.tsx`) owns navigation (spectate / quit / delete), and `pause` is blocked while `gameOver`. The hearts render in a withered palette (`lib/ui/hudPixels.ts`).
67	
68	Combat (`systems/combat.ts`) runs on the `attack` command rather than per frame: a held bow branches to `tryFireBow` (instant click-to-fire, no draw-charge), a spear melees at its longer `weaponReach`, and otherwise `tryAttackMob` swings. The `placeBlock` command runs a fixed right-click precedence (`systems/interact.ts` + engine): throw the selected spear (`tryThrowSelectedSpear`) → feed an aimed animal (`tryFeedAimedMob`) → interact with the aimed block (`tryInteractBlock`, e.g. bed/furnace/chest/door) → summon the boss from a Cursed Totem (`trySummonBoss`) → use the held item (`tryUseHeldItem`, e.g. hoe tills soil, seeds plant a crop) → `placeSelectedBlock` — so each interaction consumes the click instead of getting a block placed against it. New mechanics get a new system module and a slot in this sequence — don't grow the engine class with inline logic.
69	
```

  "new_string": "**Advancements & statistics.** Progression meta (`systems/advancements.ts`) hangs off the **single `GameEngine.emit` chokepoint** every system already emits through: `emit` pushes the event for the shell, then runs `observeProgress` — `recordEvent` folds it into the `state.stats` counters (a `Map<string, number>`), and `evaluateAdvancements` unlocks any advancement whose threshold the new stats just crossed, adding it to `state.advancements` (a `Set`) and emitting `advancementUnlocked` (the one event type `emit` skips, to avoid recursing). The advancement set is a **declarative `{ stat, threshold }` registry**, so unlock is uniform and adding one is a single row with no logic. Tick-driven display stats (distance, play time) bump directly in `step` via `recordTick`, off the unlock path. Both `stats` and `advancements` persist in save v13 and, like `xp` and unlike `effects`, are **kept across death** (`clearEffects` never touches them). The L-key overlay (`AdvancementsPanel`) reads detail through `GameApi.advancementState()` pulled on render — only the open flag and the unlocked **count** ride the snapshot, so play never churns it with stat values the HUD doesn't show.\n\nCombat (`systems/combat.ts`) runs on the `attack` command rather than per frame:"
}

```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft\ngh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main --head feat/advancements --title \"feat: advancements & statistics\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What\n\nAdds the missing **progression meta** to Monecraft. After three combat/gear releases (#35/#36/#37) the game was content-rich but goal-poor — the only objective was \"beat the Dragon Lord.\" This gives players a guided tour of every system and a running record of what they've done:\n\n- **Statistics** — running counters: blocks mined, monsters slain, items crafted/enchanted, potions drunk, fish caught, animals bred, distance travelled, time played, deaths, jumps, villager trades.\n- **Advancements** — a tight, flat set of **16** achievements, one per major system (mine a log/stone/sliver/diamond, craft a pickaxe, craft a furnace, fire a bow, slay a hostile, defeat the Dragon Lord, breed animals, harvest wheat, catch a fish, enchant an item, drink a potion, sleep, trade with a villager). Each unlocks the moment its goal is met, with a toast + chime.\n\nBoth surface in a dedicated **`L`-key overlay** (two tabs), **persist in the save and survive death** (like `xp`, unlike `effects`), and add **zero binary assets** — advancement icons reuse existing item/block sprites.\n\n## How\n\n- **One chokepoint.** Every gameplay event already funnels through `GameEngine.emit`. A single observer there folds each event into the stat counters (`recordEvent`) and unlocks any advancement whose threshold the new stats just crossed (`evaluateAdvancements` → `advancementUnlocked`) — no per-system bookkeeping. The advancement set is a **declarative `{ stat, threshold }` registry** in `lib/game/engine/systems/advancements.ts`, so adding one is a one-line edit.\n- A new broadly-useful **`crafted` event** drives the craft/brew/trade counters. `HOSTILE_MOB_KINDS` (`lib/game/mobs.ts`) is the single source of truth for counting hostile kills (hostility isn't stored on `MOB_TEMPLATES`).\n- **Persistence:** save **v12 → v13** (additive, pure migration). `WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays 8; `SAVE_KEY` unchanged.\n- **UI:** the `L` key mirrors the inventory handler; the two full-screen overlays are mutually exclusive and the pause menu won't open over either. The panel reads detail through a new `GameApi.advancementState()` pulled on render, so play never churns the snapshot with stat values the HUD doesn't show. A celebratory ZZFX chime plays on unlock.\n\n## Commits\n\n1. `feat(stats): track gameplay statistics`\n2. `feat(advancements): registry + unlock tracking`\n3. `feat(ui): advancements & statistics overlay (L key)`\n4. `docs(advancements): document advancements & statistics`\n\n## Verification\n\nFull CI `verify` gate green — `lint`, `typecheck`, `format:check`, `bun test` (900 pass), `build` — plus `bun run test:e2e` (18/18, including a new overlay test: press `L` → opens → shows the unlocked entry → switches tabs → closes). New coverage: `recordEvent`/`evaluateAdvancements` per-event and threshold/idempotence/order, registry integrity, a full save round-trip + death-survival through the engine, the v12→v13 migration + (de)serialize, and the `AdvancementsPanel` component. Docs updated: manual, reference (advancement + stats tables), save-format (v13), adding-content (extension pattern), architecture (emit-observer note), testing (coverage map), and `CHANGELOG.md`.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open PR against hutusi/main"
}

All one-seam additions; **no save bump** — new enchant ids ride per-slot `enchantments`, new effect ids ride top-level `effects`, both added to the compile-enforced `VALID_ENCHANT_IDS`/`VALID_EFFECT_IDS`. Fixed a **pre-existing** bug (user-reported): equipped armor occupied a hotbar slot because equipping was a by-reference marker (`equippedArmor[slot]=id`) leaving the item in the inventory (double-render + by-id ambiguity). Now `EquippedArmor = Record<ArmorSlot, InventorySlot|null>` (worn instances); `toggleEquipArmor` MOVES the piece out of inventory (swap-on-occupied), new `unequipArmor` moves it back (refused when full); `equippedDefense`/`armorReduction`/`consumeEquippedArmorDurability`/`mendXp` read the record directly; `syncEquippedArmor`/`unequipMissingArmor` deleted. Save **v11→v12** (`equippedArmor` persists per-slot `SavedSlot` via `serializeEquippedArmor`/`restoreEquippedArmor`; `migrateSaveV11toV12` moves legacy by-id equips out of `inventorySlots`). 28	## Status: Hardcore MERGED (2026-06-21)
29	
30	**Hardcore — `hutusi/monecraft#34` MERGED** (2026-06-21, merge commit `3559cf6`; local branch deleted, `main` synced; **remote branch NOT deleted — pending user**). The LAST gameModes.ts-deferred axis — now **nothing deferred** (game modes → difficulty → hardcore all shipped). A per-world **immutable boolean** (not an enum/accessor module — just a flag threaded through). User chose the MOST faithful variant of all 3 design Qs: **spectate-the-dead-world** permadeath (not auto-delete/simple-gameover), **lock Survival + Hard** (not difficulty-only), **withered hearts** (cosmetic yes). Save **v9→v10** (additive `hardcore` + `gameOver` booleans; `migrateSaveV9toV10`), no worldgen change, zero assets. Engine: constructor resolves `hardcore` FIRST so it overrides mode/difficulty (forced Survival+Hard); a restored `gameOver` boots Spectator (the existing `!isNoclip` stuck-check leaves it un-teleported). The TWO lethal seams (`applyDamage`/`applyEnvironmentalDamage` arrow methods — poison/starvation-floored use `applyNonLethalDamage`, can't kill) divert to new **`GameEngine.triggerGameOver()`**: sets `gameOver`, switches to spectator DIRECTLY (bypassing the locked switch), leaves `isDead`/`respawnTimer` cleared so the respawn DeathScreen never shows. `switchGameMode`/`switchDifficulty` no-op under hardcore; `pause` blocked during gameOver. Shell **force-saves on the `gameOver` event** (engine has no storage handle) so a tab-close still resumes the dead world. UI: CreateWorldForm hardcore toggle that locks the mode/difficulty grids (`disabled` + forced `effectiveMode`/`effectiveDifficulty`); PauseMenu disables both switchers; new **`GameOverScreen`** (mirrors VictoryScreen) with Spectate (minimizes to a corner badge)/Back-to-Worlds/Delete-World; new GameShell `onDeleteWorld` (deleteWorld + clear session + world-select). Withered hearts = 2 new PixelPalettes + 3 `heart_hardcore_*` HudIconNames in hudPixels.ts; StatusBars takes a `hardcore` prop + `sprite` override on the heart IconRow. 6 feature slices + a CodeRabbit-fix commit (`8f3ef37`). Gate green: lint/typecheck/format/**772 unit**/build + **17 e2e** (e2e save-assert 9→10; new menu e2e asserts hardcore boots locked survival+hard). **CodeRabbit (only reviewer) posted 2 quick-win findings, BOTH applied**: (1) `restoreGameOver` now requires `hardcore===true` too (a stray gameOver flag on a corrupt non-hardcore save can't lock it into spectator); (2) `sanitizeWorld`/`createWorld` normalize a hardcore WorldMeta's gameMode/difficulty to survival/hard (so contradictory persisted values can't leak to the world-card UI). Merged via **merge commit** (repo convention). NOT released/tagged — `[Unreleased]` now holds game modes + difficulty + hardcore. **NOTE: death→spectator visuals not headless-verifiable — a manual gameplay pass is recommended before the next release cut.**

Design: `lib/game/gameModes.ts` holds the `GameMode` union + **intent predicates** (`takesDamage`/`canEditBlocks`/`freeBuild`/`canInteract`/`canFly`/`isNoclip`/`usesInventory`/`mobsThreaten`) that each system gates on — so all four share one set of seams (damage chokepoints in `playerLife.ts`, edits in `mining.ts`, aggro in `mobAI.ts`, flight/noclip in `playerMotion.ts`). `gameMode` rides the per-world `worldType` plumbing but is the *current, switchable* value; transient `isFlying` never serialized. New commands `setGameMode`/`toggleFlight`/`creativeGiveItem`; new `FLY_SPEED`/`FLY_DOUBLE_TAP_WINDOW_SECONDS`. Creative has an all-items palette (replaces recipe book) + double-tap-Space flight. 8 feature commits + a CSS/e2e fixup + a CodeRabbit-review-fix commit (`457640d`). Gate green: lint/typecheck/format/**711 tests**/build + **16 e2e** (e2e save-version assert bumped 7→8; pause-menu CSS made scrollable since the mode selector grew it past short viewports). **CodeRabbit (CHILL, only reviewer) posted 4 findings, ALL fixed**: (1) constructor load-time auto-unstuck + switchGameMode exit now `!isNoclip`-gated incl. `y<2` (a saved Spectator inside terrain was being teleported out on load); (2) craft/swapSlots/moveStack/toggleEquipArmor/enchant dispatch gated on `canInteract` (spectator command-surface defense); (3) creative chest break skips `spillChestOnBreak` (just deletes the container — free/dropless contract); (4) pause control-hint relabeled (spectator can't *toggle* flight). Re-review on the fix commit raised 0 new. Replied to both inline threads + merged via **merge commit** (repo convention). NOT released/tagged — lands in `[Unreleased]` (a future release rolls it up). **DEFERRED to a follow-up** (user explicitly scoped them out): the **Difficulty** axis (Peaceful) + the **Hardcore** flag — both plug into the same predicate/spawn seams later. Next-feature candidates still open: companions & taming, advancements & goals, building/decoration blocks, water & exploration.

> AGENT

Done. The **Advancements + Statistics** feature is implemented end-to-end on `feat/advancements`, pushed, and opened as **[PR #38](https://github.com/hutusi/monecraft/pull/38)** against `hutusi/main`.

## What shipped (4 focused commits)

| Commit | Scope |
|---|---|
| `feat(stats)` | `state.stats` Map + `recordEvent`/`recordTick` + `STATS` + `HOSTILE_MOB_KINDS` + the new `crafted` event + save **v12→v13** persistence of stats |
| `feat(advancements)` | 16-entry declarative `ADVANCEMENTS` registry + the emit-chokepoint observer + `advancementUnlocked` event + `advancements` Set persistence |
| `feat(ui)` | `L`-key handler, `toggleAdvancements` command, `GameApi.advancementState()`, the two-tab `AdvancementsPanel` + CSS, the unlock toast + a new ZZFX chime |
| `docs` | manual, reference (advancement + stats tables), save-format (v13), adding-content (extension pattern), architecture (emit-observer note), testing (coverage), CHANGELOG |

## Design highlights
- **One chokepoint, zero per-system edits** — every event already flows through `GameEngine.emit`; a single observer there records stats and unlocks advancements.
- **Declarative registry** — each advancement is a `{ stat, threshold }` row, so `evaluateAdvancements` is pure and adding one is a one-liner.
- **Death-proof persistence** — stats + advancements survive death like `xp` (unlike `effects`), verified by a round-trip-then-die engine test.
- **Zero binary assets** — icons reuse existing item/block sprites.

## Verification (full CI gate green)
`lint` · `typecheck` · `format:check` · **`bun test` 900 pass** · `build` · **`bun run test:e2e` 18/18** (incl. a new test: press `L` → opens → shows the unlocked entry → switches tabs → closes).

The three plan refinements all held up: `HOSTILE_MOB_KINDS` replaced the non-existent `MOB_TEMPLATES.hostile`, the `crafted` event drives the trade/craft counters, and `itemIconUrl` rendered the block-id icons fine.

**One thing left to you:** a quick manual gameplay pass — the overlay layout/icons and the unlock toast + chime aren't headless-verifiable. Everything else is covered by tests. CodeRabbit review on the PR is still pending.

===== INLINE REVIEW COMMENTS (actionable findings) =====
── lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts:257
_🗄️ Data Integrity & Integration_ | _🟡 Minor_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Filter restored advancement ids against the registry.**

`restoreAdvancements()` only guarantees “non-empty string”, so a corrupt/manual-edited save can inject arbitrary ids into `state.advancements`. Because the snapshot publishes `state.advancements.size` directly, those bogus ids inflate the unlocked count and get written back out forever on the next save.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-      for (const id of restoreAdvancements(save)) this.state.advancements.add(id);
+      for (const id of restoreAdvancements(save)) {
+        if (ADVANCEMENTS_BY_ID[id]) this.state.advancements.add(id);
+      }
```
</details>

   


Also applies to: 1058-1059

in GameEngine should not add arbitrary advancement ids from
restoreAdvancements(save) directly into state.advancements; validate each
restored id against the advancements registry before adding it, so corrupt or
manually edited saves cannot inflate the unlocked count or persist bogus ids.
Update the loop in GameEngine that restores stats and advancements to filter
invalid ids using the registry-backed advancement lookup used elsewhere in the
class, and only add ids that are known and valid.
```

── lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts:725
_🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Hardcore deaths never increment the `deaths` statistic.**

This chokepoint makes emitted events the source of truth for progression, but the hardcore death path emits `gameOver` instead of `died`. `recordEvent()` only bumps `deaths` for `"died"`, so permadeath runs undercount deaths. Either emit `died` before `gameOver`, or teach the progression recorder to treat `gameOver` as a death as well.

── lib/game/engine/state.ts:275
_🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Add a stat-change invalidation key to the snapshot.**

Because Line 274 pulls panel data through `snapshot.api?.advancementState()`, the overlay only re-renders when `GameSnapshot` changes. Line 306 bumps that only on unlock count, so stat-only updates such as `play_time` and `distance_walked` can stay stale in the open Statistics tab until some unrelated snapshot field changes. A `statsVersion`/`progressVersion` that increments on every `recordEvent`/`recordTick` mutation would fix the refresh contract cleanly.

── lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts:332
_🎯 Functional Correctness_ | _🟡 Minor_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**This toast path inherits the existing stale-timeout race.**

`flashMessage()` queues a new clear timer every time without canceling the previous one. Unlocks commonly happen in the same burst as `crafted`, `pickedUp`, or autosave toasts, so an older 1200ms timer can clear this 2400ms advancement toast early, and this timer can later clear the next message. Please make the helper replace the active clear timer instead of stacking them.

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```
Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the

is stacking clear timers, so an older timeout can clear a newer toast early and
then clear the next message later. Update flashMessage in useMinecraftGame so it
replaces any active clear timer before scheduling a new one, and make sure the
advancementUnlocked path still uses flashMessage without changing its call
sites.
```

</details>

Done. Here's the summary of the code review and what I did.

## CodeRabbit review on PR #38

CodeRabbit (the only reviewer) posted **5 actionable findings** — 2 Major, 3 Minor. I assessed each against the code and **applied all 5**, committed locally as `f68231b` (**not pushed** — per your workflow, you check review fixes before push).

| # | Severity | Finding | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 🟠 Major | Hardcore deaths emit `gameOver`, not `died`, so `recordEvent` never counted them → `deaths` undercounts | `recordEvent` now treats `gameOver` as a death too (exactly one of the two fires per death, so no double-count) |
| 2 | 🟠 Major | Stats tab reads `advancementState()` on render but the snapshot only bumps on unlock → `play_time`/`distance` can read stale | `MinecraftGame` re-renders on a 400ms interval **only while the overlay is open** |
| 3 | 🟡 Minor | Restored advancement ids weren't registry-filtered → a corrupt save inflates the count & round-trips bogus ids forever | Restore loop keeps only ids in `ADVANCEMENTS_BY_ID` |
| 4 | 🟡 Minor | `items_crafted` counted station outputs (smelts/brews/trades) | Gated to non-station recipes (those have their own counters) |
| 5 | 🟡 Minor | `flashMessage` stacks clear-timers → a short toast can wipe the 2400ms unlock toast early | Cancels the prior clear timer before scheduling a new one |

**One deliberate deviation:** for #2, CodeRabbit suggested a per-frame `statsVersion` on the snapshot — I declined that specific approach because it would reintroduce exactly the per-frame snapshot churn the design exists to avoid (re-rendering the whole HUD 60×/sec). The open-only interval gets the same freshness without the regression.

**Skipped (not a fix):** CodeRabbit's docstring-coverage warning (52% < 80%) is CodeRabbit-configured, not the repo's `verify` gate — skipped per established precedent on this repo.

## Verification after fixes
Full gate re-green: **lint (0 warnings)** · typecheck · format · **902 unit tests** · build · **18 e2e**. New tests cover `gameOver`→`deaths`, station-output gating, and the registry-filter-on-load.

The fix commit sits on `feat/advancements` locally. Say the word and I'll push it (it'll update PR #38 and re-trigger CI/CodeRabbit). A manual pass on the overlay layout/icons and the unlock toast+chime is still the one thing tests can't cover.
